# Open gaps and new defects, per feature (post-wave-7 re-certification)

Generated by `merge.py` from the per-group files in this folder (2026-09-30). Code: `main` at `287cb90f7` (waves 7A–7D merged); baseline matrix at `a4d172588`.
Every item names the current `file:line`. Work top-down: FAIL first, then `[high]`, then `[medium]`.

**192 baseline features:** 7 FAIL · 2 PARKED · 0 BLOCKED · 147 PARTIAL · 15 NOT_IMPLEMENTED · 21 PASS
(before wave 7: 9 FAIL · 0 PARKED · 0 BLOCKED · 149 PARTIAL · 14 NOT_IMPLEMENTED · 20 PASS)

**63 additional per-trigger-type / channel rows (g06):** 5 FAIL · 0 PARKED · 0 BLOCKED · 45 PARTIAL · 10 NOT_IMPLEMENTED · 3 PASS

Items: **126 verified fixed**, **684 still open**, **294 new defects** (164 medium, 98 low, 17 high, 15 unrated).

## Fixed after this re-certification

- `23057af00` (workflow, user-reported hang): run-control state keys got a last-write reducer, so a failing step beside parallel branches pauses the run on that step instead of dying with LangGraph `InvalidUpdateError`; credential-free built-ins (web_search, http_request, OCR) are provisioned on first use for tenants created after startup; the legacy `helm/agentverse` worker now consumes the workflows.* and plan goals.* queues. Proven by `tests/workflow/test_parallel_failure_state.py`, `tests/mcp/test_builtin_lazy_provisioning.py` and the real-worker `tests/e2e_full/test_workflow_parallel_failure_worker_e2e.py`. Workflow run execution stays FAIL: the Celery HITL resume re-runs pre-approval steps (see below).

## Most severe new defects

- **[high]** Built-in MCP servers (registry_wiring) — app/mcp/client.py:666-690 + app/mcp/servers/*_server.py (317 of 324 handlers, e.g. hubspot_server.py:188-199, confluence_server.py:145-179, gitlab_server.py) — confused deputy / cross-tenant exposure. The guard requires the tenant to supply credentials, then dispatches handler(tool_name, arguments) without them because the handler has no `credentials` parameter, and the handler uses os.getenv('HUBSPOT_API_KEY') etc. Probe: tenant auth_config api_key='TENANT-A-KEY' with platform HUBSPOT_API_KEY set -> outgoing 'Authorization: Bearer PLATFORM-KEY-xyz', success=True. With platform env set, every tenant reads and writes the platform's vendor account. Without it, every tenant's connector fails 'not configured' (the tenant credentials are never used) [high]
- **[high]** Incident controls: emergency stop and legal holds — app/scaling/tasks.py:617 + :3361 + app/services/goal_service.py:511-541 — the worker step gate takes org_id from initial_context, which comes from _subgoal_context -> graph_context_from_execution_context. That function keeps only GRAPH_CONTEXT_KEYS (debate_*/supervisor_*) and never org_id (probe: graph_context_from_execution_context({'org_id':'org-1'}).get('org_id') -> None). So the worker gate checks only the tenant key. POST /orgs/{id}/emergency-stop (app/org/router.py:2037-2066) cancels nothing itself and promises 'running goals halt at their next step', but a running worker (production) goal of the stopped org runs to completion [high]
- **[high]** Tool reliability memory — app/agent/nodes/executor_mixin.py:1406-1424 calls get_unreliable_tools(tenant_id=..., threshold=0.3), but the signature is (*, tenant_id, min_calls, max_success_rate) (tool_reliability.py:136-138). Every call raises TypeError, which except: pass swallows, so the executor never learns which tools are unreliable [high]
- **[high]** Tool reliability memory — no production code path calls ToolReliabilityStore.record() for real tool executions: grep finds only verifier_mixin.py:646 (synthetic BLACKLIST). tool_reliability_memory therefore holds only synthetic failures, and GET /memory/tool-reliability (api/memory.py:600-607) reports those as real reliability data [high]
- **[high]** Event-bus trigger consumers (TriggerConsumerSupervisor) — triggers/store.py:181-182 + consumers/chain.py:180 + dispatcher.py:525-528 — bind_refs_to_spec copies the trigger's agent_id (the agent to RUN) into watch_agent_id, and ChainTriggerConsumer treats watch_agent_id as the SOURCE-goal filter. A goal_completed/goal_failed trigger meant as 'when any goal completes, run agent X' only fires on X's own goals, then re-fires itself on the goal it just created, up to MAX_CHAIN_DEPTH=10 times (a self-chaining loop; the helper reproduced it with a scratch script) [high]
- **[high]** External integrations: Slack, Zapier, Alertmanager/Datadog event bus, re-ingest webhooks, IMAP email — api/integrations.py:108-126 — /slack/commands still uses the single SLACK_TENANT_ID env tenant (not the workspace binding the 28cb879d5 fix uses for buttons), so with more than one bound workspace, any user in workspace B can run /agentverse and submit autonomous goals (budget, connectors, tools) into tenant A [high]
- **[high]** Messaging gateway (Telegram / WhatsApp / Slack / Teams / generic webhook, /v1/gateway) — api/channels/ingestion.py:269-272 — Teams events resolve the tenant from body.serviceUrl, a regional Bot Framework endpoint shared by every Teams organization (e.g. smba.trafficmanager.net/<region>/), so the first tenant to map that URL receives every org's Teams messages and fires its CHAT_* triggers on them [high]
- **[high]** Workflow run execution (runner, compiler, steps, Celery, run control) — workflow/runner.py:470-546 (reached from resume_from_hitl :676-695 whenever Celery is wired) — execute_resume_fresh seeds only the ONE decided step into a fresh state (step_outputs={}, runner.py:326) and re-invokes the graph from the start. Every step before the approval re-executes (e.g. an http/tool step replays its side effect), and in a workflow with two approval steps, resuming gate2 re-suspends at gate1 with a new approval request, so the run loops and never completes (reproduced: scratchpad/g06wt/probe_resume.py) [high]
- **[high]** Org mission execution, HITL approvals and brain proposals — app/org/router.py:898-911,1012-1025 POST /{org_id}/tasks/{task_id}/approve and /reject have no require_org_role and no task_kind=='approval_gate'/status check, while /approvals/{id}/approve requires TEAM_LEAD (:1386) and approval_gate kind (:1407-1412). A viewer-role key can therefore clear a human approval gate (task→running plus HITL approve_async) through the task route [severity: high]
- **[high]** Goal/tool/cost/agent/eval analytics — app/analytics/aggregator.py:128-145 (used by :164-181, :205-207, :289, :356, :397, :492) — the in-memory fallback _get_all_goals reads every tenant's goals in GoalService._goals with no tenant_id filter, and the fallback fires not only on DB error but whenever the tenant's DB result is empty (`if db_rows:` / `if results:`). Probe: tenant-A with no goals got total=1, total_cost_usd=12.5, tools ['b_private_crm.export'], agents ['b-secret-agent'] from tenant-B via goal_metrics/tool_metrics_db/agent_metrics_db — a cross-tenant leak on every new or idle tenant's /analytics/goals|tools|costs|agents [severity: high]
- **[high]** TriggerType goal_completed: firing path (event → dispatcher → goal) — triggers/store.py:181-182 + consumers/chain.py:180 + dispatcher.py:525-528 — the trigger's agent_id (the agent to run) is copied into watch_agent_id, which the chain consumer uses as the SOURCE filter. 'When any goal completes, run agent X' fires only on X's own goals and then re-fires on the goal it just created, up to MAX_CHAIN_DEPTH=10 (self-loop; reproduced by the helper's scratch script) [high]
- **[high]** TriggerType goal_failed: firing path (event → dispatcher → goal) — Same agent_id→watch_agent_id misrouting as goal_completed: triggers/store.py:181-182, consumers/chain.py:180 [high]
- **[high]** TriggerType chat_command: firing path (event → dispatcher → goal) — api/channels/ingestion.py:269-272 — Teams tenant resolved from the shared regional serviceUrl, so every Teams org's messages reach one tenant [high]
- **[high]** TriggerType chat_keyword: firing path (event → dispatcher → goal) — api/channels/ingestion.py:269-272 — Teams tenant resolved from the shared regional serviceUrl, so every Teams org's messages reach one tenant [high]
- **[high]** TriggerType chat_mention: firing path (event → dispatcher → goal) — api/channels/ingestion.py:269-272 — Teams tenant resolved from the shared regional serviceUrl, so every Teams org's messages reach one tenant [high]
- **[high]** Gateway channel Slack: inbound message → agent/goal path — api/integrations.py:108-126 — /slack/commands uses the single SLACK_TENANT_ID for every workspace, so a user of another bound workspace submits goals into that tenant [high]
- **[high]** Gateway channel Teams: inbound message → agent/goal path — api/channels/ingestion.py:269-272 — serviceUrl is a regional Bot Framework endpoint shared by every Teams organization, so whichever tenant maps it first receives ALL orgs' Teams messages and fires its triggers on them (cross-tenant data exposure) [high]

## Agent core

### [PARTIAL] HITL pause/resume and checkpoint resume  _(was FAIL)_

_The high-severity bypass is closed: a BLPOP error now raises HITLWaitUnavailableError and the waiter falls back to the local event + DB status polling, never returning PENDING (hitl.py:1146-1159, 544-576, 595-617), and the supervised write_high tool gate requires an explicit APPROVED before call_tool (executor_mixin.py:2315-2337, 23e47c7d3). Every supervised gate now denies non-APPROVED. Non-supervised modes still file approval requests nobody waits on, and the approval row is persisted fire-and-forget._

- [ ] NEW app/governance/hitl.py:254-264 — request_approval (the sync call every executor gate uses) persists the approval row with an unreferenced fire-and-forget task whose errors are swallowed; if that write fails the request exists only in this worker's memory, the approver's replica (DB-first aget_request) cannot see or approve it, and a supervised goal waits the full HITL timeout and then fails [medium]
- [ ] Non-supervised modes file approval requests nothing waits on: action-safety HITL_REQUIRED files and the step runs executor_mixin.py:969-1006; policy REQUIRE_APPROVAL files then denies executor_mixin.py:1151-1165; HITL gate 7 files and runs executor_mixin.py:1188-1215; tool gate files and returns a 'requires approval' text executor_mixin.py:2289-2375
- [ ] wait_for_approval looks the request up only in the process-local dict (unknown id → REJECTED) hitl.py:518-520 (low; callers wait on requests they created)
- 2 item(s) verified fixed (see matrix)

### [PARTIAL] Goal-tree decomposition  _(was PARTIAL)_

The other 317 handlers take no `credentials` parameter and read platform env vars (e.g. hubspot_server.py:188-199). _dispatch_builtin_tool checks that the tenant supplied its own credentials and then calls handler(tool_name, arguments) without them (app/mcp/client.py:666-690). The guard requires the tenant to supply credentials, then dispatches handler(tool_name, arguments) without them because the handler has no `credentials` parameter, and the handler uses os.getenv('HUBSPOT_API_KEY') etc. Probe: tenant auth_config api_key='TENANT-A-KEY' with platform HUBSPOT_API_KEY set -> outgoing 'Authorization: Bearer PLATFORM-KEY-xyz', success=True. With platform env set, every tenant reads and writes the platform's vendor account. Without it, every tenant's connector fails 'not configured' (the tenant credentials are never used) [high]

### [PARTIAL] Code interpreter and sandbox execution  _(was PARTIAL)_

_Not changed by wave 7. The Docker sandbox re-verified for real on colima (network_mode=none, read_only, uid 1000, pids_limit, cap_drop=ALL, no-new-privileges, deadline kill, always removed; app/tools/code_interpreter.py:193-247). The unsandboxed fallback is blocked in production and off by default elsewhere (271-300). The output cap is still applied after the full log read, audit failures are swallowed, and there is no concurrency bound._

- [ ] NEW app/api/tools.py:28-64 + app/tools/code_interpreter.py:258 — no per-tenant or global concurrency cap on /tools/execute-code. Each call holds a default-executor thread for up to 60 s (run_in_executor(None, ...)), so one tenant with a tools:write key can fill the replica's default thread pool. That pool is also what assert_public_url_async / PinnedNetworkBackend use via asyncio.to_thread, so every tenant's outbound SSRF-checked calls on that replica stall, and there is no host-level cap on concurrent containers [medium]
- [ ] NEW app/tools/code_interpreter.py:284 — the production block compares ENVIRONMENT == 'production' case-sensitively (other checks lower() it, e.g. app/api/connectors.py:118), so ENVIRONMENT=Production plus AGENTVERSE_ALLOW_SUBPROCESS_EXEC=true would run unsandboxed code [low]
- [ ] The output cap is applied after container.logs() has read the whole stream into API memory (app/tools/code_interpreter.py:238-243)
- [ ] Audit failures on /tools/execute-code are swallowed with a warning only, and nothing is recorded when app.state.audit_log is None (app/api/tools.py:67-99)

### [PARTIAL] A2A protocol and agent directory  _(was PARTIAL)_

_No commits touched app/api/a2a.py, agent_directory.py or the a2a_tasks migration since the baseline (a4d172588..HEAD). The honest parts hold: tasks run as the authenticated caller, signatures are timestamped and single-use (Redis SET NX, fail-closed 503), inbound is refused in production without a secret, and callbacks are re-validated and IP-pinned. But execution is still a fire-and-forget task on the API replica, callbacks are unsigned, context is dropped, and a hard 300s watcher timeout permanently records long goals as 'timeout'._

- [ ] NEW app/api/a2a.py:25,69-77,404-411 — the outcome watcher has a hard 300s timeout and then writes a terminal 'timeout' status and fires the callback. The goal keeps running: any HITL-gated or long research goal later completes, but the A2A task is never updated. No goal_id is stored (task_data at 380-388 has none, and the 0023 schema has no column), so the calling agent cannot reconcile. Result: false-negative outcomes for every goal longer than 5 minutes [medium]
- [ ] NEW app/api/a2a.py:305-310 — A2ATaskRequest has no length bounds. goal is unbounded, whereas POST /goals caps it at 10_000 (app/api/goals.py:54), so A2A bypasses the input cap. Oversized callback_url or requester_agent_id overflow String(500)/String(255) (0023_a2a_tasks.py:18-19), and the INSERT in _persist_task (a2a.py:198-212) fails with a 500 instead of a 422 [low]
- [ ] NEW app/api/a2a.py:128,164-169 — when app.state._redis is absent, the replay cache is a per-process dict. On multi-replica deployments without Redis, a captured signed request can be replayed once per replica within the 10-minute TTL [low]
- [ ] Fire-and-forget execution: execute_and_callback (submit_goal + outcome watch) is asyncio.create_task on the API replica; a restart or deploy leaves the a2a_tasks row 'accepted' forever with no callback, and nothing reconciles it (app/api/a2a.py:396-414)
- [ ] Per-agent directory is still an honest 501 (app/api/agent_directory.py:26-37)
- [ ] Callback POST is unsigned (no HMAC/timestamp header), so the receiver cannot authenticate it (app/api/a2a.py:290-299)
- [ ] Agent card advertises only X-A2A-Signature, not X-A2A-Timestamp or the '{ts}.'+body signing base, so a spec-following client gets 401 (app/api/a2a.py:322-326 vs 142-151)
- [ ] A2ATaskRequest.context is accepted but never passed to submit_goal (execution_context not set) (app/api/a2a.py:307, 398-403)
- [ ] If app.state.goal_service is None the task is persisted and answered 202 'accepted' but never executed (app/api/a2a.py:390-420) [low]
- [ ] Synchronous assert_public_url (blocking DNS) called inside async handlers stalls the event loop on slow resolvers (app/api/a2a.py:285, 369) [low]
- [ ] No test covers restart/lost-task recovery, long-running (>300s) goals, or a signed callback. The Postgres tenant-boundary e2e (tests/e2e_full/test_a2a_tenant_boundary_e2e.py) is BLOCKED because Docker/colima must not be started in this run

_Wave 7A closed the main holes. Supervised approval now fails closed on anything but APPROVED, and a Redis error during the wait falls back to DB polling. Slack HITL takes the tenant from the verified workspace binding. ### [PARTIAL] Audit trail (AuditLog, audit_log table, SIEM forwarding)  _(was PARTIAL)_

Inserts scheduled by the final step(s) are still pending then and are destroyed or run against a disposing engine, so the last tool-execution audit rows of worker goals (the production path) can be lost with no log at all. ### [PARTIAL] Incident controls: emergency stop and legal holds  _(was FAIL)_

_ed55a2fb5 (wave 7A) makes the tenant e-stop real. The flag has no TTL and POST/DELETE/GET return 503 without Redis. Running goals are enumerated from Postgres and cancelled through the cross-replica path. The stop is enforced at submit (409/503), at AgentGraph start and at every step boundary on the API and the worker (fail closed on a Redis error, proven by tests). Org e-stop requires ORG_ADMIN. Legal holds are unchanged and still work. But the org stop is not enforced at worker step boundaries, the worker start check fails open, and every worker goal start SCANs the whole Redis keyspace._

- [ ] NEW app/scaling/tasks.py:617 + :3361 + app/services/goal_service.py:511-541 — the worker step gate takes org_id from initial_context, which comes from _subgoal_context -> graph_context_from_execution_context. That function keeps only GRAPH_CONTEXT_KEYS (debate_*/supervisor_*) and never org_id (probe: graph_context_from_execution_context({'org_id':'org-1'}).get('org_id') -> None). So the worker gate checks only the tenant key. POST /orgs/{id}/emergency-stop (app/org/router.py:2037-2066) cancels nothing itself and promises 'running goals halt at their next step', but a running worker (production) goal of the stopped org runs to completion [high]
- [ ] NEW app/governance/emergency_stop.py:52-61 + app/scaling/tasks.py:1962-1966 — every worker goal start (when no tenant stop is set) runs redis.scan_iter(match='emergency_stop:{tenant}:*', count=100). SCAN MATCH walks the entire Redis keyspace (goal locks, cancel flags, cost counters, 24 h idempotency keys ...), so each goal start costs O(total keys)/100 round-trips; at millions of goals this serialises worker starts on Redis. The org keys should be a per-tenant SET or looked up by the goal's org_id [medium]
- [ ] NEW app/scaling/tasks.py:3346-3352 + :613-623 — multi_agent workflow goals on the worker run _WorkerWorkflowRunner, which exposes no _pause_gate, so no e-stop step-boundary check is installed. The tenant stop reaches them only through the per-goal cancel flag set by the enumeration (lost if active_goal_ids fails, governance.py:1387-1391), and an org stop never stops them [medium]
- [ ] NEW app/api/governance.py:1383-1398 — the e-stop request enumerates every non-terminal goal of the tenant with no LIMIT (goal_service.py:2109-2116) and cancels them sequentially inside the HTTP request. For a tenant with a large queued backlog the call times out, although the flag already blocks them [low]
- [ ] e-stop audit entry is fire-and-forget, so audit_recorded=True means only queued -> app/api/governance.py:1442-1470 via AuditLog.record (app/governance/audit.py:89-95) [low]
- [ ] worker start-of-goal check fails OPEN on a Redis/DB error: app/scaling/tasks.py:1957-1986 wraps emergency_stop_reason_sync (which, unlike enforce_emergency_stop_sync, does not catch errors) in 'except Exception: logger.warning' and proceeds. AgentGraph goals are then caught at the first step gate, but multi_agent workflow goals are not (see new defects) (wave-7A follow-up, not done) [medium]
- [ ] 'celery_signal_sent': True is hard-coded in the response (app/api/governance.py:1485) whatever happened [low]

### [FAIL] Tool reliability memory  _(was PARTIAL)_

## Workflows and triggers

### [FAIL] Workflow run execution (runner, compiler, steps, Celery, run control)  _(was FAIL)_

_The in-process HITL gate bypass is fixed (c2f04e29f): approval steps route through one decision router, and a barrier guard stops join steps (the re-run probe now shows step_outputs=[] while waiting). But the production Celery path is still broken. resume_from_hitl dispatches execute_resume_fresh, which rebuilds the run from its inputs with empty step_outputs and re-runs the graph from the first step. A scratch probe (pre → gate1 → mid → gate2 → after) showed: resuming gate1 re-executed 'pre'; resuming gate2 re-executed 'pre' and 'gate1' and created a NEW gate1 approval. So pre-approval side effects replay on every approval, and any workflow with two approval steps never completes._

- [ ] NEW workflow/runner.py:470-546 (reached from resume_from_hitl :676-695 whenever Celery is wired) — execute_resume_fresh seeds only the ONE decided step into a fresh state (step_outputs={}, runner.py:326) and re-invokes the graph from the start. Every step before the approval re-executes (e.g. an http/tool step replays its side effect), and in a workflow with two approval steps, resuming gate2 re-suspends at gate1 with a new approval request, so the run loops and never completes (reproduced: scratchpad/g06wt/probe_resume.py) [high]
- [ ] NEW workflow/steps/hitl_step.py:120-122 — without hitl_workflow_gateway it silently enters 'test mode' (no approval record) and suspends forever [low]
- [ ] stream_run_events is a one-shot snapshot that emits 'run_failed' for any non-terminal run → workflow/service.py:478-497
- [ ] Per-process unbounded compiled-graph cache → workflow/compiler.py:29 (self._cache dict, cleared only wholesale at :39)
- [ ] Worker runs use MemorySaver, so there is no durable mid-run checkpoint (see 'Durable execution') → workflow/celery_tasks.py:36,123
- 2 item(s) verified fixed (see matrix)

### [FAIL] Workflow HITL approvals (approval inbox)  _(was FAIL)_

_The in-process fail-open gate is fixed (c2f04e29f): downstream steps no longer run while an approval is pending, and reject stops the run. The SLA escalation sweep and tenant-scoped delegate/escalate still hold, and the Docker-backed workflow HITL and cross-replica visibility e2e tests pass. But those e2e tests use a single-step HITL workflow. On the production Celery resume path, approving re-runs every pre-approval step, and a second approval step re-creates the first approval, so multi-approval workflows never finish. Decisions have no assignee/role authorization and are not atomic across replicas._

- [ ] NEW workflow/hitl_extension.py:240-270 + workflow/router_hitl.py:135-150 — decide() records any authenticated tenant principal as reviewer; it never checks req.assigned_to or assigned_role, so an approval assigned to a specific approver or role can be decided by any user or API key in the tenant [medium]
- [ ] NEW workflow/hitl_extension.py:258-275 + workflow/approval_store.py:104-121 — decide is a read (status=='pending'), then an unconditional upsert (ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE SET status=EXCLUDED.status), then resume. Two reviewers on different replicas can both pass the pending check, one approving and one rejecting; last write wins and BOTH resume callbacks dispatch execute_workflow_run [medium]
- [ ] round_robin (the default strategy) and skill_based assignment return None → workflow/hitl_extension.py:637-639,653-655
- [ ] least_busy counts only the process-local _store, so assignment differs per replica → workflow/hitl_extension.py:641-651 [low]
- [ ] The Celery cross-process resume replays from scratch (see 'Workflow run execution'): runner.py:470-546 re-executes pre-approval steps and re-suspends at earlier approval steps [high]
- 2 item(s) verified fixed (see matrix)

### [PARTIAL] Durable execution / LangGraph checkpointing  _(was PARTIAL)_

_Unchanged by wave 7. API-side DB checkpoint resume works (tests pass), but Celery workers, which run every queued production goal and workflow run, still use MemorySaver. Now that 2d8ea71f7 lets a crashed worker's 'executing' goal be reclaimed on redelivery, the redelivered run starts from step 0 and re-executes any side-effecting tools the dead run already completed._

- [ ] Workers never get a durable checkpointer → scaling/tasks.py:91,94-111 _WORKER_CHECKPOINTER stays None (MemorySaver); passed at tasks.py:2939-2940 and workflow/celery_tasks.py:36,123
- [ ] SIGTERM handler claims a durable checkpoint → scaling/tasks.py:29-43 logs 'LangGraph checkpoint written after last completed step', which is false for MemorySaver
- [ ] A sync-only or unusable saver is silently swapped for MemorySaver → agent/graph.py:269-282 (no log or metric)
- [ ] A crash-redelivered goal re-runs from scratch → tasks.py:1836-1838 lets a crashed worker's executing goal be reclaimed, with no checkpoint to resume from, so completed tool calls are replayed (no idempotency) [medium]

### [PARTIAL] Trigger/schedule CRUD and ScheduleStore  _(was PARTIAL)_

_The high-severity multi-replica defect is fixed (5a6d78ecf). Every /schedules handler now reads and writes Postgres through the *_async store methods with strict=True, maps an outage to 503, pauses/resumes durably through set_paused_async, and resolves webhook tokens from the schedules.webhook_token column. test_schedules_cross_replica.py proves the two-replica cases. Still open: NL creation skips validation and LLM charging, there is no plan cron floor, list pagination happens in Python, and some other callers still read only the per-process cache._

- [ ] NEW api/schedules.py:446 — nl_create_schedule calls nl.parse(body.command) without tenant_ctx, so complete_decision gets tenant=None and skips both the budget preflight and the charge (providers/guarded_completion.py:132-133,168-169); every NL schedule LLM call is unmetered despite the 7D fix to nl_scheduler.py [medium]
- [ ] NEW api/agents.py:925-931 — deleting an agent cleans up its schedules from schedule_store.list_all() (the per-process cache), so schedules for that agent created on another replica survive and keep firing goals for a deleted agent [medium]
- [ ] NEW triggers/store.py:747-749,806-811 — every DB read writes each row into the per-process self._data dict, which is never bounded or evicted, so each API replica eventually caches every schedule it has served (unbounded memory at million-schedule scale) [low]
- [ ] NEW chat/service.py:839-860 — the chat SCHEDULE intent persists LLM-parsed specs through create_async with no is_supported/validate_spec and no quota_plan, so a chat turn can create triggers of unsupported types (s3_event, mqtt, …) that never fire, and can bypass PLAN_MAX_TRIGGERS [medium]
- [ ] POST /nl/schedule stores nl.parse() specs without is_supported/validate_spec → api/schedules.py:446-462; nl_scheduler.py:522 last-resort ONCE spec has no fire_at_iso, so it is stored and never fires
- [ ] validate_cron has no plan-tier minimum interval (the docstring claims one) and skips validation entirely without croniter → triggers/models.py:246-258
- [ ] GET /schedules loads every tenant schedule and slices in Python → api/schedules.py:189-190 (limit/offset not pushed to SQL); triggers/store.py:726-743 has no LIMIT [low]
- [ ] /triggers API reads non-strict: a DB outage silently falls back to the per-process cache instead of 503 → api/triggers.py:48-54,265-267 (get_async/list_all_async without strict), and :340 re-reads the created record from the sync cache [low]
- 2 item(s) verified fixed (see matrix)

### [PARTIAL] TriggerDispatcher governance pipeline  _(was PARTIAL)_

_No wave-7 commit touches triggers/dispatcher.py. The pipeline is real: two-layer dedup that fails closed on Redis errors, fail-closed conditions, audited skip rows, and enqueue failure goes to the DLQ. The Docker-backed replay-dedup and governed-schedule e2e tests pass. Still true: the RBAC step is a no-op, circuit breakers are per process, and on the Celery beat path the breaker is rebuilt on every fire so it can never open. Rate limiting and the bulkhead fail open when Redis errors._

- [ ] NEW scaling/tasks.py:3871-3891 + :3787-3793 — every run_scheduled_goal builds a fresh TriggerDispatcher (so a fresh CircuitBreakerRegistry, dispatcher.py:80), so the per-trigger circuit breaker can never trip for beat-fired triggers and a persistently failing schedule keeps dispatching every tick [medium]
- [ ] NEW triggers/rate_limiter.py:43-55 and triggers/bulkhead.py:28-43 — both return True (allow) on a Redis error or when redis is None, so during a Redis blip a free tenant's triggers bypass the 10/h cap and the 2-slot bulkhead (fail open), unlike the dedup gate, which fails closed [low]
- [ ] caller_role defaults to 'operator' and no caller passes it, so the RBAC step always passes → triggers/dispatcher.py:91,153,168 (no caller_role= argument anywhere in app/)
- [ ] CircuitBreakerRegistry is an in-process dict (per replica) → triggers/circuit_breaker.py:76-84; the bulkhead is released right after enqueue → dispatcher.py:376-377, so it bounds concurrent dispatch calls, not in-flight goals
- [ ] The Redis dedup key (SET NX, 60s) is claimed before the rate-limit, circuit and bulkhead checks, so a firing skipped as rate_limit/bulkhead_full/circuit_open burns the key and a legitimate redelivery within 60s is deduped → dispatcher.py:209-252,405-409 [low]

### [PARTIAL] Beat schedule loop (time family A plus polling/data triggers)  _(was PARTIAL)_

_Unchanged by wave 7 apart from 7D's SSRF pinning of the API-poll and RSS fetches (fdc81e6a5). Beat fires go through run_scheduled_goal → TriggerDispatcher, and interval, cron, missed-run and Family A logic is tested. Still true: the RRULE/SOLAR branches are dead, each tick does O(all schedules) work, and Postgres discovery is off by default. New finding: schedule payloads never carry the tenant's plan, so every beat-fired trigger is dispatched and run as FREE. Two more beat gaps: a CEL condition set via condition_cel is dropped, and three Family A configs pass validation but can never fire._

- [ ] NEW triggers/store.py:216-237 + scaling/tasks.py:3665,3778-3782 — neither the Redis schedule payload nor the DB payload contains tenant_plan, so every beat fire runs with PlanTier.FREE. An enterprise tenant's schedules are rate-capped at 10 fires/hour (rate_limiter.py PLAN_CAPS), limited to 2 bulkhead slots and a 64 KB payload, and the resulting goals get free-tier queue and timeout [medium]
- [ ] NEW scaling/tasks.py:3722-3743 + :3662 — _build_scheduled_trigger_spec and _enqueue_governed_fire forward only sched['condition']. The API stores condition_cel as condition_expression (api/triggers.py:172), so a beat-fired trigger (cron/interval/rss/api_poll…) with a CEL condition fires unconditionally: its condition gate is silently dropped (fail open) [medium]
- [ ] NEW triggers/validation.py:61-71 vs scaling/tasks.py:4205-4232 — validate_spec accepts relative_delay with only relative_to_field (or an offset with no fire_at_iso), deadline with only deadline_field, and business_calendar with only business_calendar_id. The beat needs fire_at_iso or cron_expression and never reads those fields, so these triggers are accepted and never fire [medium]
- [ ] NEW triggers/validation.py:45-46 — expires_at_iso is validated but never enforced by the beat loop, the dispatcher or any consumer (no other reader in app/), so an 'expired' trigger keeps firing [medium]
- [ ] rrule/solar branches are unreachable because RRULE/SOLAR are not TriggerType members → scaling/tasks.py:4930-4960 (rrule) and :4165-4200 (_solar_due_run_utc); triggers/models.py:28-100
- [ ] Each tick SCANs every schedule:* key (one GET each), and with DB discovery on, runs one query per active tenant with no due-time predicate → scaling/tasks.py:4789-4801, :4365-4398 [medium]
- [ ] Postgres discovery is opt-in, so Redis is the de-facto source of truth and an evicted schedule:* key stops firing until an API restart re-syncs → scaling/tasks.py:4435-4443 (AGENTVERSE_DB_SCHEDULE_DISCOVERY defaults to 'false'), :4816-4819 [medium]

### [PARTIAL] Event-bus trigger consumers (TriggerConsumerSupervisor)  _(was PARTIAL)_

_No wave-7 change. Six core consumers (chain, hitl, memory, event, condition, conversational) start when Redis is available, publishers exist for goal chain, HITL, memory.created (API) and state_machine.transition (API), and the N-replica fan-out is deduped by the dispatcher. MQTT is still never wired, and the worker publishes no memory.created events. New finding: every consumer's pub/sub loop dies on the first Redis error and is never restarted._

- [ ] NEW triggers/store.py:181-182 + consumers/chain.py:180 + dispatcher.py:525-528 — bind_refs_to_spec copies the trigger's agent_id (the agent to RUN) into watch_agent_id, and ChainTriggerConsumer treats watch_agent_id as the SOURCE-goal filter. A goal_completed/goal_failed trigger meant as 'when any goal completes, run agent X' only fires on X's own goals, then re-fires itself on the goal it just created, up to MAX_CHAIN_DEPTH=10 times (a self-chaining loop; the helper reproduced it with a scratch script) [high]
- [ ] NEW triggers/consumers/event.py:36-38,118 (same at condition.py:137, conversational.py:256) — publish_trigger_event merges the client body before stamping tenant_id, so a client-supplied 'tenant_plan' survives, and the consumer uses it as the dispatch plan. A free tenant POSTing {'tenant_plan':'enterprise'} to /triggers/events/{ch} gets the enterprise rate cap, bulkhead, payload limit and goal queue [medium]
- [ ] NEW triggers/consumers/chain.py:96-107 (same pattern at condition.py:77-84, conversational.py:214-221, event.py:72-79, hitl.py:38-45, memory.py:38-45) + triggers/supervisor.py:136-142 — each consumer's `async for message in pubsub.listen()` sits inside one try/except that logs and returns, and _run_consumer never restarts it. A single Redis failover or connection reset permanently kills every event-driven trigger family on that replica, with no health signal, until the pod restarts [medium]
- [ ] NEW triggers/consumers/*.py — the event bus is Redis pub/sub (at-most-once), so events published while a replica's consumers are down or reconnecting (deploys, the failure above) are lost with no replay or DLQ; chain, HITL and memory triggers silently miss firings [low]
- [ ] NEW triggers/consumers/condition.py:63-66,173-174 — COUNTER_THRESHOLD/WINDOW_AGGREGATE/STATE_TRANSITION keep their counters, windows and last state in a per-process dict, and every replica receives every event, so each replica counts independently and state is lost on restart (counter thresholds fire N times or never) [medium]
- [ ] MQTT consumer is always skipped → triggers/supervisor.py:205-216 (mqtt_client = None, 'not wired by default', and only built when the extended flag is on)
- [ ] Worker-written memories never publish memory.created → scaling/tasks.py:2541 LongTermMemoryStore() with no set_event_redis (memory/long_term.py:94-97, so _event_redis stays None)
- [ ] state_machine.transition is published only by POST .../transition → api/state_machines.py:171-199; transitions made through StateMachineStore elsewhere publish nothing
- [ ] goal_score_below has no proven production publisher: the only publish is GoalService's in-process eval path (services/goal_service.py:2616-2631); the Celery worker publishes only goal.completed/goal.failed (scaling/tasks.py:2051-2080), and no test drives score_below for a worker-run goal (unverified whether it ever fires in production)

### [PARTIAL] Inbound webhook and channel trigger ingress (triggers/schedules/channels)  _(was PARTIAL)_

_The legacy /schedules/webhooks/{token} ingress now resolves the token from the durable schedules.webhook_token column, so it works across replicas (5a6d78ecf, proven by test_schedules_cross_replica). The typed path (/triggers/webhooks/{type}/{token}) still resolves the tenant pre-auth from the token, verifies GitHub and Stripe correctly, and answers 503 when every dispatch fails. Still open: the pre-auth O(N) cache scan, non-strict DB reads, and vendor signature schemes other than GitHub/Stripe are wrong._

- [ ] NEW api/triggers.py:802-810 + triggers/webhooks/verifier.py:96-106,19-39 — slack (and teams) typed webhooks read the generic 'x-signature' header and check HMAC(body), but Slack signs 'v0:{X-Slack-Request-Timestamp}:{body}' in X-Slack-Signature. A SLACK_EVENT trigger with a signing secret therefore 401s every real Slack delivery; without a secret it has no auth beyond the token, and the url_verification challenge is never echoed, so Slack cannot enable the endpoint [medium]
- [ ] NEW api/triggers.py:70-83 — _webhook_tenant_ctx takes the plan from tenant_service._tenants (the per-process dict), falling back to FREE, so a tenant not cached on this replica has its webhook goals governed and run as free tier [low]

_Slack HITL buttons now decide in the tenant bound to the signature-verified workspace (team id → channel_tenant_mappings; unbound workspaces are ignored) and record the approver as slack:<uid> (28cb879d5). The remaining defects: the Slack slash command still submits into one env tenant for every workspace; every integration hard-codes PROFESSIONAL; Alertmanager/Datadog bypass the dispatcher and ack failures with 200; Slack approvals have no approver authorization; IMAP turns any inbound email into an autonomous goal._

_Router-wide org ownership (app/org/router.py:127-128 _verify_org_ownership) and the fail-closed viewer default in app/org/rbac.py:80-117 hold, and org CRUD tests pass. Wave 7 only touched approvals/e-stop/LLM metering in this package; attachments are still host-local, org_artifacts is still unused, and the viewer fail-closed role is enforced on only 11 of 40 mutating routes._

- [ ] NEW app/org/router.py:445,500,603,631,762,844,1080,2442,2928,3105,3461,3746,3800,3816,3876 — mutating org routes have no require_org_role although rbac.py:96-99 says a key without an org role resolves to viewer and 'is denied'; e.g. POST /missions requires TEAM_LEAD (:532) but POST /missions/execute (:3461, creates AND dispatches a mission) and POST /missions/batch (:2928) need no role, so a viewer-role key launches billable missions [severity: medium]
- [ ] NEW app/org/router.py:1932-1995 custom-role create/update/delete have no role check, and OrgRoleStore is only ever listed (no enforcement reads the permissions), so custom roles are decorative metadata any tenant key can rewrite [severity: low]

### [PARTIAL] Org mission execution, HITL approvals and brain proposals  _(was PARTIAL)_

_f24fa00ee now records every org decision under the authenticated key (_decision_actor, app/org/router.py:876-887) rather than the body's approver, and HITL votes stay DB-first across replicas. The gate-wiring swallow, the unbounded KEYS scan and the keyword preview remain. The task-level approve/reject routes bypass the TEAM_LEAD gate that the approvals routes enforce._

- [ ] NEW app/org/router.py:898-911,1012-1025 POST /{org_id}/tasks/{task_id}/approve and /reject have no require_org_role and no task_kind=='approval_gate'/status check, while /approvals/{id}/approve requires TEAM_LEAD (:1386) and approval_gate kind (:1407-1412). A viewer-role key can therefore clear a human approval gate (task→running plus HITL approve_async) through the task route [severity: high]

## Services and reliability

### [PARTIAL] Goal lifecycle service (GoalService)  _(was PARTIAL)_

_3960746c1 makes cancel/pause conditional (WHERE status NOT IN terminal). A goal that finished meanwhile keeps and reports its real status, and a failed write is a 503 (goal_service.py:4836-4930, 5606-5656; tests/services/test_goal_status_transitions.py). ff36dadf4 makes GET /eval read persisted evaluations/eval_scorecards under RLS, answers 503 on a DB error, and bounds the cache with an LRU (goal_service.py:4585-4700, 615). Still open: goal dedup races, the eval endpoints still gate on the memory-only _get_record, and the HITL resume/reject status writes are unconditional and swallow DB errors._

- [ ] NEW app/services/goal_service.py:5166-5168,5200-5202 — resume_goal's reject (FAILED) and approve (EXECUTING) writes call _db_update_goal_status with neither only_if_active nor raise_on_error. A DB error is swallowed while the API answers 'rejected'/'resumed' and dispatches goal_failed/goal_resumed. For a suspended supervised goal (no runner to write the terminal state) a lost reject write leaves the row WAITING_HUMAN forever. This is the fake-success class 3960746c1 fixed for cancel/pause but left here [medium]
- [ ] NEW app/services/goal_service.py:4852-4870 — cancel sends the Redis cancel signal (_signal_runner) BEFORE the conditional DB write, so when the write fails the caller gets 503 while the runner still cancels the goal (reported failure, action applied) [low]
- [ ] Dedup still races: get_existing (goal_service.py:3838-3843) and register (goal_service.py:3869-3876) are separate calls, register()'s False (SET NX lost) is ignored, and errors hit a bare 'except Exception: pass', so concurrent identical submits both run
- [ ] get_eval/get_eval_suggestions/run_eval still start with the memory-only _get_record (goal_service.py:4673, 4715, 4778+), so the new persisted read is unreachable for any goal not in this replica's memory. That includes every goal evicted 1h after completion (_COMPLETED_GOAL_TTL_SECONDS=3600, goal_service.py:123) and goals older than the sync_from_db window, which get 404 instead of their persisted scorecard. tests/services/test_eval_persisted_read.py only covers a goal submitted to the same service [medium]
- [ ] is_valid_transition is still imported with noqa F401 and never enforced → goal_service.py:96
- [ ] Every status write sets iterations=0 (the default at goal_service.py:5613,5636), so cancel/pause wipe the recorded iteration count [low]
- [ ] Restart recovery only scans self._goals as warmed by sync_from_db (last 24h, capped per tenant) → goal_service.py:963-1060 [low]
- 4 item(s) verified fixed (see matrix)

### [PARTIAL] SSE goal event delivery and cross-replica fanout  _(was PARTIAL)_

_No SSE code changed in the range. The local path still registers its queue before replay, and the Celery bridge treats worker_failed and worker_complete as terminal (goal_service.py:824-840). The cross-replica subscribe path (goal_service.py:5254-5319) and the SSE endpoint (api/goals.py:745-765) are unchanged, so every prior gap and defect remains; 27 tests pass._

- [ ] SSE id is event._seq or a local counter; live/queued events carry no _seq, so Last-Event-ID drifts → app/api/goals.py:749-761
- [ ] Resume replay is capped at list_events_since's default limit=100 (event_store.py:132); _list_events_since_persisted passes no limit and does not paginate → goal_service.py:2381-2393
- [ ] The cross-replica path replays persisted events (goal_service.py:5264-5270) BEFORE pubsub.subscribe (goal_service.py:5298), losing events published in between; it returns silently when no Redis URL is set (goal_service.py:5285-5286)
- [ ] No heartbeat or is_disconnected check on the goal SSE stream, so a WAITING_HUMAN goal holds an idle connection indefinitely → app/api/goals.py:745-765
- [ ] Bridge stub GoalRecords (created_at='') are created for every unknown goal_id (goal_service.py:777-800). The bridge sets the terminal status but never completed_at (goal_service.py:836-840), and _evict_stale_goals skips records without completed_at (goal_service.py:871-879), so stubs grow without bound
- [ ] The cross-replica live loop stops only on goal_complete/goal_failed/goal_cancelled; worker_failed (timeout, lock failure, crash) never matches, so the stream hangs → goal_service.py:5302-5308 [medium]
- [ ] Every API replica psubscribes goal_events:* for ALL tenants and creates a stub per goal, so per-replica memory and CPU scale with fleet-wide event volume → goal_service.py:764 [medium]
- [ ] The cross-replica path yields the worker's raw envelope {goal_id, tenant_id, type, payload}, not the local event shape → goal_service.py:5299-5304 [low]

### [PARTIAL] Durable goal event store  _(was PARTIAL)_

_event_store.py is unchanged. Sequences are allocated race-free by UPDATE goals.event_seq ... RETURNING in the same statement as the INSERT (event_store.py:55-67). The API and the worker (tasks.py:2096) both append through it. A final append failure and a missing goal row are still only logged, and a list_events DB error is still replayed as an empty history. The one unit test passes; a Postgres-backed concurrency test was not run (Docker not used by this audit)._

- [ ] append_event swallows the final failure after 3 retries (event_store.py:85-91), and a missing goal row (rowcount 0) is only logged (event_store.py:76-84), so events can silently vanish from the durable stream
- [ ] list_events has no try/except (event_store.py:93-126); GoalService._list_persisted_events catches and returns [] (goal_service.py:2369-2379), and list_events_since returns [] on error (event_store.py:170-174), so a DB outage replays an empty history as if the goal had no events
- [ ] Replay queries filter on goal_id/sequence but not on the created_at partition key, so every replay probes every monthly partition → event_store.py:115-124,152-160 [low]

### [PARTIAL] Goal dedup and idempotency  _(was PARTIAL)_

_No change to reliability/idempotency.py, services/dedup.py or the submit handler in the range. Idempotency-Key claim/complete/release with a fail-closed 503 and goal_id replay still work (api/goals.py:188-262; 16 passed). Goal dedup is still a non-atomic check-then-register with a 60s TTL, and a missing idempotency store still fails open._

- [ ] Goal dedup race: get_existing (goal_service.py:3838-3843) then register (goal_service.py:3869-3876) is non-atomic; register()'s False is ignored and exceptions are swallowed
- [ ] RedisDeduplicationCache still keys on per-process hash(goal) (reliability/dedup.py:76,84,90); unused in production [low]
- [ ] Idempotency-Key replay is not bound to the request body, so reusing a key with a different body returns the first response → api/goals.py:216-219 [low]

### [PARTIAL] Cross-process goal lifecycle signals and execution locks  _(was PARTIAL)_

_2d8ea71f7 adds an atomic claim after the per-goal lock: UPDATE goals SET status='executing' WHERE status NOT IN terminal RETURNING id, under RLS. A redelivered finished goal is skipped, and an unverifiable claim retries and then fails closed (tasks.py:1825-1870, 2230-2270; tests/scaling/test_run_goal_claim.py). The broker visibility_timeout now outlasts the longest plan timeout (celery_app.py:333-343). Strict remote signalling, the Redis pause gate and a lock TTL of timeout+300s remain. There is still no lock renewal, paused time still counts toward the timeout, and signal read errors still hide a cancel._

- [ ] NEW app/scaling/celery_app.py:333-343 — broker_transport_options.visibility_timeout (about 25h) is global to every task on the Redis broker, so the acks_late crash recovery the claim relies on only redelivers after about 25h. Meanwhile detect_stuck_goals (tasks.py:5531+) fails the goal, so a worker crash never actually resumes a goal; the late redelivery just hits 'already_terminal'. This fails honestly, but crash recovery exists only on paper [low]
- [ ] _SyncGoalLock has no extend/renewal (tasks.py:133-148); correctness depends on wait_for(goal_timeout_s) (tasks.py:3354-3365) firing before the TTL of timeout+300s (tasks.py:2191-2196)
- [ ] check_pause_cancel still has no callers (reliability/goal_lifecycle.py:155+) and GoalExecutionLock (reliability/distributed_lock.py) is unused [low]
- [ ] is_paused/is_cancelled (and the _sync variants) return False on Redis read errors, so a Redis blip hides a cancel from a runner → reliability/goal_lifecycle.py:121-152
- [ ] Plan timeout includes paused time: a worker goal paused longer than its plan timeout (1h on free) is killed as 'Goal timed out' (worker_failed) although the pause flag lives 7 days (goal_lifecycle.py:36,79) → tasks.py:3354-3368 [medium]
- [ ] No lock when the broker is not Redis and REDIS_URL is unset: _redis_url='' skips the lock with no warning → tasks.py:2166-2172 [low]
- 1 item(s) verified fixed (see matrix)

### [PARTIAL] Circuit breakers  _(was PARTIAL)_

_redis_circuit_breaker.py and the executor breaker code are unchanged. The MCP client's async per-connector Redis breaker and complete_decision's per-model breaker plus timeout (now covering many more call sites via 340f40776) still apply, and an open circuit fails the step honestly (88 passed). The executor breaker still uses the sync in-memory fallback, HALF_OPEN still has no TTL, and the 7B worker graph rebuild still passes no circuit_breakers._

- [ ] The executor picks get('llm') or get(tool_name), so connector breakers are never consulted there and tool-step failures are recorded on the 'llm' breaker. It calls sync can_call/record_*, which on RedisCircuitBreaker delegate to a per-instance in-memory fallback, so the Redis state is never read or written from the executor → app/agent/nodes/executor_mixin.py:1036-1044,1621,1634
- [ ] OPEN->HALF_OPEN is a SET without TTL (redis_circuit_breaker.py:122-125). HALF_OPEN then returns False fleet-wide forever if the prober never reports (redis_circuit_breaker.py:129-133)
- [ ] The worker graph services dict (tasks.py:2912-2951) still has no circuit_breakers, so Celery-run goals get no executor-level breaker (MCP-client and provider breakers still apply) [low]
- [ ] The provider breaker is a module-level per-process singleton, so each replica and worker learns about a dead provider independently → app/providers/circuit_breaker.py:69 [low]

### [PARTIAL] Bulkhead (per-tenant concurrency)  _(was PARTIAL)_

_The Celery worker graph now receives a RedisBulkheadRegistry (tasks.py:1751-1764, 2946-2948; 9e03570d5), so worker-run goals count against the same distributed per-tenant bulkhead as the API. This is tested by tests/scaling/test_worker_runtime_profile.py::test_worker_graph_gets_the_tenant_bulkhead. bulkhead.py is unchanged: the atomic Lua acquire/release, the degrade-to-local-or-deny on Redis errors, and the executor failing closed still hold (14 + 6 passed). Limits are still a flat 20, never plan-derived, and the slot-TTL issues remain._

- [ ] NEW app/scaling/tasks.py:1751-1764 — _worker_bulkhead_registry() builds a new RedisBulkheadRegistry (and a new redis.asyncio client/pool, never closed) for every run_goal. Its in-process fallback semaphore is therefore per goal, so when Redis is unreachable the worker's 'degrade to local limit' does not limit a tenant across its goals, and each goal leaves an unclosed connection pool behind [low]
- [ ] available_slots_sync returns max, and the registry's available_slots reads the local registry → reliability/bulkhead.py:139-141,197-198 [low]
- [ ] configure_tenant limits live in a per-process _limits dict and nothing outside bulkhead.py calls configure_tenant, so every tenant gets 20 regardless of plan (main.py:2052-2055, tasks.py:1760) [medium]
- [ ] EXPIRE on every acquire (bulkhead.py:81) keeps leaked slots (crashed holder, or a release error swallowed at bulkhead.py:143-144) alive while traffic continues, and a step longer than _SLOT_TTL=300s lets the key expire mid-flight, resetting the count and allowing more than the limit
- 1 item(s) verified fixed (see matrix)

_Wave 7C (c0f713faa) added customer.subscription.updated and invoice.payment_failed handling. Both read the LIVE subscription from Stripe (503 if unreachable, so Stripe retries) and set free for past_due/unpaid/canceled/incomplete_expired/paused or restore metadata.plan for active/trialing (app/api/billing.py:745-825,893-904). update_plan writes Postgres and invalidates the shared Redis caches. - [ ] Mock order/verify and demo invoices behind allow_mock_payments/development → app/api/billing.py:249-265,492-519,572-596 (create-order writes a mock order even when allow_mock_payments is False; verify then 503s)

### [PARTIAL] Insights API  _(was FAIL)_

_35d7c73c6 (wave 7C) rewrote estimate, agent-health, query and benchmarks as SQLAlchemy Core over ORM tables. _app/api/memory_v2.py is unchanged since a4d172588. The DB is authoritative, with RLS on every path, 503 on errors and FOR UPDATE on PATCH/DELETE/consolidate. Conflicts are still a process-local, unbounded dict. Wave 7D's MEMORY_WRITE screening (e8d4dfe7b) covered app/api/memory.py only. /memory-v2 writes unscreened rows into the same long_term_memory table that agent recall reads, and those rows stay recallable after a 'GDPR' soft delete._
